title: Piping valves catalog
topic: synoptics
summary: Eight hand and self-acting piping valves with symbol, real working, states, property names and simple model.
tool: none
version: "none"
checked: 2026-11-13

# Piping valves catalog

Contents: 1. Symbol shape · 2. Isolation valves · 3. Fine and check valves · 4. Pressure valves · Sources

Actuated valves (motor, hydraulic, solenoid, control) and the three-way valve, strainers and manifold are in `catalog-actuated-valves.md`. Roles and shared sets: `docs/synoptics/fluid-common.md`. Laws: `docs/synoptics/fluid-model-laws.md`.

## 1. Symbol shape (piping valves)

- Standards, cited by reference: ASTM F1000 for ship piping [S3], ISO 14617-8 [S2], ISO 10628-2 [S4].
- A two-way valve is two triangles point to point; a three-way valve adds one triangle. [S50]
- An actuator is marked by a letter; project rule: draw the actuator above the valve. [S50]
- Write `NO` or `NC` as text for the normal state. Never use the fill as the only state code. [S50]
- The mark of each valve type changes between standards: follow the legend of the project.

## 2. Isolation valves (piping catalog)

### Gate valve (vanne à opercule)

- Symbol: two triangles point to point, with no mark or a vertical line at the center. [S3] [S2] [S50]
- Real working: a wedge gate lifts out of the flow and leaves an opening almost equal to the pipe; used open or closed, never to throttle; handwheel. [S9] [S15]
- States: `closed`, `opening`, `open`, `closing`, plus `hasPositionFault`.
- Properties: set `onOffValve`; a hand valve without sensor keeps only `isOpen` (boolean, M), set by the operator or the instructor.
- Model: open, a small pressure loss proportional to flow; closed, zero flow or a set leak. [S34]

### Globe valve (robinet à soupape)

- Symbol: two triangles point to point with a filled disk at the center. [S3] [S2] [S50]
- Real working: a disk closes on a seat and its position sets the flow; the most common stop valve on board, known by its inner build, not its outside. [S9] [S15]
- States: `closed`, `opening`, `open`, `closing`, `intermediate`.
- Properties: set `onOffValve` when it isolates, set `modulatingValve` when it throttles.
- Model: `Q = Kv × f(opening) × √(Δp / relative density)`. [S33]

### Ball valve (vanne à boisseau sphérique)

- Symbol: two triangles point to point with a circle at the center. [S3] [S2] [S50]
- Real working: a bored ball turns a quarter turn, open or closed in 90 degrees; large sizes have a gearbox, easier but slower. [S9] [S15]
- States: `closed`, `opening`, `open`, `closing`.
- Properties: set `onOffValve`.
- Model: on or off, as the gate valve. [S34]

### Butterfly valve (vanne papillon)

- Symbol: a line with a tilted disk, or two triangles point to point with an oblique line. [S3] [S2] [S50]
- Real working: a disk turns 90 degrees on a shaft; light, compact, fast, it can throttle; lever, or air, electric or hydraulic actuator on large sizes. [S9] [S15]
- States: `closed`, `opening`, `open`, `closing`, `intermediate`.
- Properties: set `onOffValve` or `modulatingValve`.
- Model: on or off [S34], or the Kv law when it throttles [S33].

## 3. Fine and check valves (piping catalog)

### Needle valve (vanne à pointeau)

- Symbol: two triangles point to point with an arrowhead at the center. [S3] [S50]
- Real working: a long tapered point enters a small seat for a very fine flow setting; it protects delicate gauges and slows an end of travel. [S9]
- States: continuous opening.
- Properties: `openingPercent` (number, %, P or C).
- Model: `Q = Kv(opening) × √(Δp / relative density)`. [S33]

### Swing and lift check valve (clapet à battant et à levée)

- Symbol: two triangles point to point, one filled, or an arrow for the free direction. [S3] [S4] [S50]
- Real working: it passes one way and closes the other, with no command. Swing type: horizontal lines, low resistance. Lift type: where flow often reverses, no slam, more resistance. A stop-check valve isolates or checks, by the stem position. [S9] [S15]
- States: `closed`, `open`; stop-check valve also `shut`.
- Properties: `isOpen` (boolean, X); `crackingPressureBar` (number, bar, P); `isStemClosed` (boolean, C or M, stop-check valve).
- Model: zero flow in reverse; else an orifice. [S34]

## 4. Pressure valves (piping catalog)

### Safety and relief valve (soupape de sûreté et de décharge)

- Symbol: an angle valve with a spring above it. [S3] [S4]
- Real working: a safety valve (gas, steam) pops fully open; a relief valve (liquid) opens with the overpressure; it lifts at a preset pressure and reseats when pressure falls; pilot and vacuum versions exist. [S31] [S15]
- States: `closed`, `lifted`.
- Properties: `setPressureBar` (number, bar, P); `isLifted` (boolean, M or X); `hasLiftedAlarm` (boolean, D).
- Model: safety valve, on or off with hysteresis; relief valve, `Q = K × max(0, p − setPressureBar)`.

### Pressure reducing valve (détendeur)

- Symbol: two triangles point to point with a diaphragm above, linked downstream. [S3] [S50]
- Real working: it holds a steady outlet pressure; the spring type opens fully if its diaphragm breaks. [S15] [S9]
- States: `regulating`, `fullyOpen`, `closed`, plus `hasDiaphragmFault`.
- Properties: `outletPressureSetpointBar` (number, bar, C); `inletPressureBar`, `outletPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `hasDiaphragmFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: `outlet pressure = min(inlet pressure, setpoint)`; with a diaphragm fault, outlet = inlet.

## Sources

- [S2] Wikipedia, "ISO 14617", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO_14617>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S3] ASTM International, "F1000-21 Standard Practice for Piping System Drawing Symbols", 2021, <https://store.astm.org/f1000-21.html>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.
- [S4] ISO, "ISO 10628-2:2012 Diagrams for the chemical and petrochemical industry, Part 2: Graphical symbols", <https://standards.iteh.ai/catalog/standards/iso/8bc45867-650c-4d1c-b32f-726b336223ba/iso-10628-2-2012>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.
- [S9] US Navy, "Fluid Power", NAVEDTRA 14105A, chapter 6 "Valves", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-valves-navy>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S15] US Navy, "Pumps, Valves, and Piping", NAVEDTRA 14104A, chapter 9, <https://www.cedengineering.com/userfiles/Overview,%20Installation%20and%20Maintenance%20of%20Pumps,%20Valves%20and%20Piping.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S31] Wikipedia, "Safety valve", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety_valve>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S33] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid User's Guide, Valve characteristics", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.UsersGuide.ComponentDefinition.ValveCharacteristics.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S34] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid.Valves", Modelica Standard Library 4.0.0, <https://doc.modelica.org/Modelica%204.0.0/Resources/helpDymola/Modelica_Fluid_Valves.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S50] Tameson, "Valve Symbols in Process and Instrumentation Diagrams", <https://tameson.com/pages/valve-symbols-pid>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Propulsion and steering catalog

Contents: 1. Prime movers · 2. Drive train · 3. Propellers · 4. Steering and thruster · 5. Submarine plant · Sources

No public standard of propulsion symbols was found: every "usual shape" is an analysis, to fit to the legend of the project. The model equations come from an open-access article on a patrol vessel with two diesels, gears and controllable-pitch propellers; check them in the article before reuse. [S23] Every card also takes the common inputs of `symbol-property-naming.md`.

## 1. Prime movers (propulsion catalog)

### Diesel engine (moteur diesel)

- Symbol: usual shape, a rectangle with the machine name, linked to the shaft.
- Real working: large merchant ships have a slow two-stroke diesel coupled to the propeller; medium-speed four-stroke diesels drive through a gear, often several of them [S41]; a governor meters fuel to hold speed [S21]; in the published model the governor is a PI controller on the speed error and the fuel is capped by charge air pressure and speed [S23].
- States: `stopped`, `starting`, `running`, `loaded`, `stopping`, `shutdown`.
- Properties: `isStartCommanded`, `isStopCommanded` (boolean, C); `speedRpm`, `speedSetpointRpm` (number, rpm, M and C); `fuelIndexPercent`, `loadPercent` (number, %, M); `torqueKnm` (number, kN·m, M); `lubeOilPressureBar`, `chargeAirPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `coolantTemperatureDegC`, `exhaustTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `isShutdownActive` (boolean, M); `hasOverspeedFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: PI governor, capped fuel, engine torque, shaft equation (shaft line card); temperatures and pressures first-order toward a value set by the load. Public names: Signal K `propulsion.*`. [S2]

### Gas turbine (turbine à gaz)

- Symbol: usual shape, a trapezoid linked to the shaft or to an alternator.
- Real working: common on warships, often with diesels for cruising [S41]; lighter and smaller than a diesel of the same power, efficient only near full power [S45]; in combined diesel and gas, the diesel cruises (about 20 knots) and the turbine gives high speed (about 30 knots) [S48].
- States: `stopped`, `starting`, `idle`, `loaded`, `coolingDown`, plus `isTripped`.
- Properties: `isStartCommanded`, `isStopCommanded` (boolean, C); `speedRpm` (number, rpm, M); `powerKw` (number, kW, M); `powerSetpointPercent` (number, %, C); `exhaustTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `lubeOilPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `isTripped` (boolean, M).
- Model: timed start sequence; torque first-order toward the demand; exhaust temperature set by the load.

### Steam turbine (turbine à vapeur)

- Symbol: usual shape, a trapezoid with steam inlet and outlet to the condenser.
- Real working: mostly replaced since about 1960, still used on nuclear ships and some LNG carriers [S41]; steam expands in the turbine, sea water condenses it, and the water returns to the steam generator; the turbine drives the propeller through a gear, or an alternator for propulsion motors (turbo-electric) [S47] [S41].
- States: `stopped`, `warmingUp`, `running`, plus `isTripped`.
- Properties: `speedRpm` (number, rpm, M); `throttlePositionPercent`, `throttleSetpointPercent` (number, %, M and C); `steamPressureBar`, `condenserPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `steamTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `isTripped` (boolean, M).
- Model: power = throttle opening × steam pressure, with a first-order delay.

### Propulsion motor (moteur électrique de propulsion)

- Symbol: usual shape, a circle marked "M" on the shaft line, fed by its converter.
- Real working: in integrated electric propulsion, gas turbines or diesel sets make three-phase power for the propeller motors; the electric transmission replaces clutches and gears and frees the engine layout; the ship is quieter, the machines being decoupled from the hull [S45]; a drive sets the motor speed [S62].
- States: `stopped`, `ready`, `running`, `limited`, plus `hasMotorFault`.
- Properties: `isConverterReady` (boolean, M); `speedRpm`, `speedSetpointRpm` (number, rpm, M and C); `torqueKnm` (number, kN·m, M); `powerKw` (number, kW, M); `currentA` (number, A, M); `windingTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `direction` (enum `ahead`, `astern`; M); `hasMotorFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: speed control with a torque limit; power = torque × speed, a load of the main switchboard. Public names: IEC 61850 ZMOT, ZCON. [S6]

## 2. Drive train (propulsion catalog)

### Shaft line (ligne d'arbres)

- Symbol: usual shape, an axis line from the machine to the propeller, bearings marked.
- Real working: the shaft carries the power to the propeller; the thrust bearing passes the propeller thrust to the hull. [S41]
- States: `stopped`, `turning`, `locked`.
- Properties: `shaftSpeedRpm` (number, rpm, M); `shaftTorqueKnm` (number, kN·m, M); `shaftPowerKw` (number, kW, M); `thrustN` (number, N, M); `thrustBearingTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `isShaftLocked`, `isBrakeApplied` (boolean, M); `inertiaKgM2` (number, kg·m², P).
- Model: `2π × J × dn/dt = gear output torque − line losses − propeller torque`, J the total inertia at the shaft, engine, gear, line, propeller and entrained water [S23]; a public library has the same component [S24].

### Reduction gear (réducteur)

- Symbol: usual shape, a rectangle with two gear wheels between machine and shaft.
- Real working: it lowers the shaft speed for medium or high speed engines [S41]; in combined plants, multi-ratio gears serve diesel alone or diesel with turbine, about 7.7 and 5.3 in one published case [S48]; the published model removes a loss torque that depends on speed and torque [S23].
- States: `disengaged`, `engaging`, `engaged`.
- Properties: `gearRatio` (number, P); `inputSpeedRpm`, `outputSpeedRpm` (number, rpm, M); `isClutchEngaged` (boolean, M); `isClutchEngageCommanded` (boolean, C); `lubeOilPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `lubeOilTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `hasLowOilPressureAlarm` (boolean, D).
- Model: output speed = input speed / ratio; output torque = input torque × ratio − losses. Public names: Signal K `propulsion.*.transmission`. [S2]

## 3. Propellers (propulsion catalog)

### Fixed-pitch propeller (hélice à pas fixe)

- Symbol: usual shape, two spindle-shaped blades at the end of the shaft.
- Real working: fixed blades: cheaper, sturdier and more efficient than a controllable-pitch propeller at one speed and load [S42]; going astern needs reversing the shaft.
- States: `stopped`, `ahead`, `astern`.
- Properties: `speedRpm` (number, rpm, M); `thrustN` (number, N, M); `torqueKnm` (number, kN·m, M); `diameterM` (number, m, P); `pitchRatio` (number, P).
- Model: thrust and torque = coefficient × ½ × density × speed² × disk area, coefficients from open-water curves by advance angle. [S23]

### Controllable-pitch propeller (hélice à pas variable)

- Symbol: usual shape, a propeller with a curved arrow on one blade.
- Real working: the blades turn on their axis to change pitch; a negative pitch gives astern without reversing the shaft; on large ships a hydraulic system sets the blades; pitch is set to absorb the available engine power at any speed. [S42]
- States: `ahead`, `zeroPitch`, `astern`, plus `hasPitchFault`.
- Properties: `pitchPercent`, `pitchSetpointPercent` (number, −100 to 100 %, M and C); `isPitchPumpRunning` (boolean, M); `pitchOilPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `pitchTimeConstantS` (number, s, P); `hasPitchFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: `d pitch / dt = (setpoint − pitch) / τ` with rate limits; published case τ = 1.67 s, pitch decrease limited to 10 % per second; thrust and torque depend on pitch and speed. [S23]

## 4. Steering and thruster (propulsion catalog)

### Steering gear (appareil à gouverner)

- Symbol: usual shape, a rudder seen from above with a graduated sector.
- Real working: hydraulic rams fed by electric pumps turn the rudder; a position feedback stops the motion at the ordered angle [S43]; the main gear must move the rudder from 35° one side to 35° the other, and from 35° to 30° the other side in 28 seconds at most; the auxiliary gear from 15° to 15° in 60 seconds at most [S51].
- States: `stopped`, `followUp`, `nonFollowUp`, `local`, plus `hasHydraulicFault`.
- Properties: `rudderAngleDeg`, `rudderAngleSetpointDeg` (number, °, M and C); `steeringMode` (enum, M); `isPowerUnit1Running`, `isPowerUnit2Running` (boolean, M); `isPowerUnit1StartCommanded`, `isPowerUnit2StartCommanded` (boolean, C); `hydraulicPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `rudderRateDegPerS` (number, °/s, P); `hasPowerFailure`, `hasHydraulicFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: the angle joins the setpoint at constant rate, stops at ±35°; 65° in 28 s gives at least 2.3° per second with the main gear (analysis); no motion with no pump running. French official terms: [S68].

### Bow thruster (propulseur d'étrave)

- Symbol: hull mark, a cross in a circle above the waterline over the thruster [S44]; on a synoptic, a propeller in a transverse tunnel.
- Real working: a propeller in a transverse tunnel below the waterline pushes water to one side to turn the ship at berth; mostly electric; less effective when the ship moves ahead [S44]; the power management system can block the start of a large consumer [S11].
- States: `stopped`, `ready`, `running`, plus `hasOverloadFault`.
- Properties: `isStartCommanded`, `isStopCommanded` (boolean, C); `isRunning`, `isReady`, `isStartBlocked` (boolean, M); `thrustPercent`, `thrustSetpointPercent` (number, −100 to 100 %, positive to starboard, M and C); `motorPowerKw` (number, kW, M); `motorCurrentA` (number, A, M); `hasOverloadFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: thrust first-order toward the setpoint, times a factor that falls with ship speed; a large electrical load on the switchboard.

## 5. Submarine plant (public level only)

### Submarine propulsion plant (propulsion d'un sous-marin)

- Symbol: no public symbol; reuse the generator set, battery, propulsion motor and shaft line cards.
- Real working: diesel-electric, the diesels run surfaced or snorkeling to charge the batteries; submerged, a battery-fed motor drives the propeller; the snorkel is a retractable tube that brings air to the diesels at periscope depth [S46]; air-independent propulsion (Stirling engine, fuel cell, closed-cycle diesel or steam turbine with stored oxygen) recharges the batteries or feeds the motor [S49]; nuclear, a pressurized water reactor heats a primary loop, a steam generator feeds turbines that drive the propeller through a gear or make power for propulsion motors [S47]. Most submarines have one propeller, some a pump-jet [S46].
- States: `surfaceDiesel`, `snorkeling`, `battery`, `airIndependent`.
- Properties: `propulsionMode` (enum, M); `isSnorkelRaised`, `isSnorkelValveOpen`, `isChargingBattery`, `isAirIndependentPlantRunning` (boolean, M); `batteryStateOfChargePercent` (number, %, M); `mainMotorSpeedRpm`, `mainMotorSpeedSetpointRpm` (number, rpm, M and C); `airIndependentPowerKw` (number, kW, M).
- Model: energy balance on the battery (charge by diesels or the air-independent source, discharge by the motor and ship services); a state machine on the propulsion mode; no figures, they are not public.

# Ship fluid circuits and submarine tanks catalog

Contents: 1. Fuel and lube oil · 2. Cooling, bilge and ballast · 3. Submarine tanks · 4. Submarine air · Sources

The circuits are built from `catalog-ship-equipment.md` and the valve catalogs. Roles and shared sets: `docs/synoptics/fluid-common.md`. Potable water and steam have no public source in this base yet, except the steam trap.

## 1. Fuel and lube oil circuits (ship circuits catalog)

### Fuel oil system

French: circuit combustible.

- Role: receive, store, transfer and treat fuel, and feed the engines. [S21]
- Flow path: transfer from heated storage tanks through strainers to settling tanks; treatment through heater, purifier, clarifier and service tank; supply through feed pumps and flow meter, booster pumps, heaters, viscosity controller and final filter to the engine. [S21]
- Properties: `serviceTankLevelPercent` (number, %, M); `fuelSupplyPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `fuelTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `fuelViscosityCst` (number, cSt, M); `hasLowFuelPressureAlarm` (boolean, D).
- Model: tank levels by volume balance; temperatures by first-order delay; supply pressure present when a pump runs and the path is open.

### Lubricating oil system

French: circuit d'huile de graissage.

- Role: feed oil to the engine bearings and cooling oil to the pistons. [S22]
- Flow path: sump tank, pump (two, one on standby), thermostatic valve and cooler, full-flow duplex filter, bearings and pistons, crankcase, back to the tank; in parallel a cleaning centrifuge and a make-up from a storage tank. [S22]
- Properties: `lubeOilPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `lubeOilTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `sumpLevelPercent` (number, %, M); `filterDifferentialPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `hasLowLubeOilPressureAlarm` (boolean, D); `isStandbyPumpRunning` (boolean, M).
- Model: pressure from the running pump and the filter clogging; the standby pump starts under a pressure threshold; the thermostatic valve sets the temperature.

## 2. Cooling, bilge and ballast circuits (ship circuits catalog)

### Central cooling water system

French: refroidissement eau de mer et eau douce.

- Role: send the heat of engines and auxiliaries to the sea without sea water in the machines. [S20]
- Flow path: three subsystems: sea water (sea chest [S19], central cooler, overboard), low temperature fresh water (oil, charge air and jacket water coolers), high temperature fresh water (jackets, cylinder heads, exhaust valves); a fresh water generator uses the jacket water heat. [S20]
- Properties: `seaWaterInletTemperatureDegC`, `lowTemperatureWaterDegC`, `highTemperatureWaterDegC` (number, °C, M); `seaWaterPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `expansionTankLevelPercent` (number, %, M); `hasHighTemperatureWaterAlarm` (boolean, D).
- Model: each exchanger uses the effectiveness law; each fresh water loop warms with the engine load and cools with the exchange.

### Bilge system

French: assèchement.

- Role: remove the water that gathers at the lowest point of the ship, oily from machinery leaks, and treat it before discharge. [S18]
- Flow path: bilge wells, bilge pump or eductor [S15], oily water separator, oil content monitor, overboard or back to a tank, sludge tank. [S18]
- Properties: `bilgeWellLevelPercent` (number, %, M); `hasBilgeHighLevelAlarm` (boolean, D); `isOverboardValveOpen` (boolean, M); `sludgeTankLevelPercent` (number, %, M).
- Model: well level by volume balance with a set leak flow; alarm above a threshold.

### Ballast system

French: ballastage.

- Role: lower the center of gravity, correct trim and list, increase draft, spread hull loads; ballast water treatment by filtration and ultraviolet light, with a management plan and record book required since 2017. [S17]
- Flow path: sea chest, pumps, double-bottom and side ballast tanks, overboard; the public source gives neither piping nor valves. [S17]
- Properties: per tank, `levelPercent` (number, %, M) and `volumeM3` (number, m³, X); `ballastOperation` (enum `idle`, `filling`, `emptying`, `transferring`; C); `isTreatmentUnitRunning` (boolean, M).
- Model: volume balance per tank; pump flow shared between the tanks whose valve is open.

## 3. Submarine tanks (public level only)

Sources: a 1946 navy manual on "fleet type" submarines and two encyclopedia articles. Values are historical and describe no current vessel. [S16] [S17] [S52]

### Main ballast tank (ballast)

- Symbol: no public standard of its own; ASTM F1000 has a submarine figure limited to acoustic quieting parts [S3]. Proposed shape: a tank with a level line, a vent at the top and an opening at the bottom.
- Real working: main ballast tanks are outside the pressure hull; full of water they cancel the positive buoyancy, empty they give it back; to dive, the vents at the top open, air goes out and water enters from below; to surface, the vents close and compressed air drives the water out from below. [S16] [S17]
- States: `empty`, `flooding`, `flooded`, `blowing`.
- Properties: `waterFillPercent` (number, %, X or M); `ballastState` (enum, X); plus the properties of its vent valve and its blow valve.
- Model: vent open, `waterFillPercent` rises to 100; vent closed and blow open, it falls to 0; else it holds. Two rate parameters.

### Vent valve (purge)

- Symbol: the hydraulically operated valve of `catalog-actuated-valves.md`.
- Real working: vents are hydraulically operated valves with a hand back-up; emergency vents exist on most tanks; most main ballast tanks flood through free openings without a valve; other tanks have flood valves near the keel. [S16] Opening the vents lets water into the tank. [S52]
- States: `closed`, `opening`, `open`, `closing`, plus `hasPositionFault`.
- Properties: set `onOffValve`; `isHandOperated` (boolean, M); `isHydraulicPressureAvailable` (boolean, M).
- Model: a ramp over `travelTimeS`; no motion without hydraulic pressure, except by hand.

### Trim and depth control tanks (caisse d'assiette et régleur)

- Symbol: tank, pump and valve manifold cards.
- Real working: forward and aft trim tanks balance the boat lengthwise by moving water between them; central tanks fine-tune weight against buoyancy; in the 1946 manual these variable tanks have no direct sea connection: water goes through a trim manifold and service air empties them. [S52] [S17] [S16]
- States: per tank `low`, `normal`, `high`; per operation `idle`, `pumpingForward`, `pumpingAft`, `flooding`, `pumpingToSea`.
- Properties: set `tank` per tank; `trimOperation` (enum; C); `transferFlowRateM3PerH` (number, m³/h, M); `transferredVolumeM3` (number, m³, X).
- Model: volume balance per tank; the pump flow leaves the source tank and joins the destination tank.

## 4. Submarine air (public level only)

### HP air bank and blowing (air haute pression et chasse)

- Symbol: the air flask card of `catalog-compressed-air.md` and a valve for the blow valve.
- Real working: a compressed air reserve, refilled by compressors, empties the ballast tanks to surface [S52]. In the 1946 manual: a 600 psi manifold (about 41 bar) blows the main ballast tanks; a 10 psi low pressure blower (about 0.7 bar) finishes on the surface; 3,000 psi air (about 207 bar) blows special tanks; 225 psi service air (about 15.5 bar) empties variable tanks. [S16]
- States: blow valve `closed`, `open`; air bank `low`, `normal`, `full`.
- Properties: `airBankPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `isBlowValveOpenCommanded` (boolean, C); `isBlowValveOpen` (boolean, M); `isBlowing` (boolean, X); `hasLowAirPressureAlarm` (boolean, D).
- Model: blowing lowers the bank pressure in proportion to the water volume blown out (ideal gas); no effect when the bank pressure is under the depth pressure.

## Sources

- [S3] ASTM International, "F1000-21 Standard Practice for Piping System Drawing Symbols", 2021, <https://store.astm.org/f1000-21.html>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.

- [S16] US Navy, Bureau of Naval Personnel, "The Fleet Type Submarine", 1946, chapter 4 "Tank Arrangements", <https://maritime.org/doc/fleetsub/chap4.php>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S17] Wikipedia, "Ballast tank", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballast_tank>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S18] Wikipedia, "Oily water separator (marine)", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oily_water_separator_(marine)>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S19] Wikipedia, "Sea chest (nautical)", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_chest_(nautical)>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

# Ship fluid equipment catalog

Contents: 1. Pumps and eductor · 2. Tanks and exchangers · 3. Treatment · 4. Sea chest and trap · Sources

Symbol standards: ASTM F1000 for ship piping [S3]; ISO 14617-9, -11 and -12 for pumps, exchangers and separators [S2]; ISO 10628-2 for process devices [S4]. Cited, never copied. Roles and shared sets: `docs/synoptics/fluid-common.md`. The circuits built from these parts are in `catalog-ship-circuits.md`.

## 1. Pumps and eductor (ship equipment catalog)

### Centrifugal pump (pompe centrifuge)

- Symbol: a circle with a tangent discharge; the motor is a second circle beside it. [S3] [S2]
- Real working: an impeller throws the liquid outward; pressure drops at the eye and draws liquid in; the volute turns speed into pressure; a check valve at the discharge stops reverse flow; it must be primed, and poor priming often explains no flow. [S15]
- States: `stopped`, `starting`, `running`, `stopping`, plus `hasTripFault`.
- Properties: sets `drive` and `controlMode`; `suctionPressureBar`, `dischargePressureBar` (number, bar, M); `flowRateM3PerH` (number, m³/h, M); `isPrimed` (boolean, X); `hasLowSuctionPressureAlarm` (boolean, D).
- Model: head-flow curve at rated speed, scaled by the affinity laws [S35] [S36] [S40]; the operating point is where it meets the network curve `Δp = K × Q²`.

### Positive displacement pump (pompe volumétrique)

- Symbol: the pump circle with an inner mark for the type, gear or screw. [S3] [S2]
- Real working: each turn moves a fixed volume, so a relief valve must protect the circuit; it suits viscous liquids at low speed; screw pumps serve fuel, lube oil and hydraulics. [S15]
- States: `stopped`, `running`, plus `hasTripFault`.
- Properties: sets `drive` and `controlMode`; `flowRateM3PerH` (number, m³/h, M); `dischargePressureBar` (number, bar, M); `isRelieving` (boolean, X).
- Model: `Q = k × speed`, whatever the pressure; the network gives the pressure, capped by the relief valve.

### Eductor (éjecteur)

- Symbol: a converging then diverging nozzle body with a side suction. [S3]
- Real working: no moving part; a fast jet of sea water lowers the pressure in a chamber and the atmosphere pushes the water to remove into it; used to drain bilges and compartments. [S15]
- States: `off`, `running`.
- Properties: `motivePressureBar` (number, bar, M); `isMotiveFlowOn` (boolean, X); `suctionFlowRateM3PerH` (number, m³/h, X or M).
- Model: suction flow = `k × motive flow` while the motive pressure is above a threshold; else zero.

## 2. Tanks and exchangers (ship equipment catalog)

### Ship tank (caisse)

- Symbol: a rectangle with a level line. [S4]
- Real working: heavy fuel storage tanks have heating coils; settling tanks let water and solids drop; the service tank feeds the engine; the lube oil sump tank sits in the double bottom under the engine. [S21] [S22]
- States: `empty`, `low`, `normal`, `high`, `overflow`.
- Properties: set `tank`; `contentType` (enum `fuelOil`, `lubeOil`, `seaWater`, `freshWater`, `bilgeWater`, `sludge`; P); `isHeatingOn` (boolean, C or M).
- Model: `dV/dt = inflows − outflows`; `level = V / area`; bottom pressure = liquid height × density × g. [S14] [S37]

### Plate heat exchanger (échangeur à plaques)

- Symbol: a rectangle or a circle crossed by a broken line. [S3] [S2]
- Real working: the central cooler is one or two large titanium plate exchangers where sea water cools the fresh water of the inner circuits; the lube oil cooler works with a thermostatic valve. [S20] [S22]
- States: no discrete state; `fouled` when fouled.
- Properties: `hotInletTemperatureDegC`, `hotOutletTemperatureDegC`, `coldInletTemperatureDegC`, `coldOutletTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `differentialPressureBar` (number, bar, M).
- Model: hot outlet = hot inlet − effectiveness × (hot inlet − cold inlet); effectiveness zero when the cold flow is zero.

### Fuel heater (réchauffeur)

- Symbol: the exchanger symbol; the navy list marks heat input with triangles. [S4] [S7]
- Real working: before purification, a thermostatic heater brings heavy fuel to about 80 °C; before the engine, heaters bring the fuel to temperature under a viscosity controller. [S21]
- States: `off`, `heating`.
- Properties: `outletTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `temperatureSetpointDegC` (number, °C, C); `isHeatingOn` (boolean, M).
- Model: first-order delay of the outlet temperature toward the setpoint while heating.

## 3. Treatment (ship equipment catalog)

### Centrifugal purifier (séparateur centrifuge)

- Symbol: a stylized bowl on a vertical shaft. [S4] [S2]
- Real working: the purifier removes water and solids from fuel; a clarifier follows; clean fuel goes to the service tank; on lube oil, a centrifuge cleans the oil in a loop. [S21] [S22]
- States: `stopped`, `running`, plus `hasSeparatorFault`.
- Properties: set `drive`; `inletTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `throughputM3PerH` (number, m³/h, M); `hasSeparatorFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: constant throughput while running; water content falls with running time.

### Oily water separator (séparateur d'eaux mazouteuses)

- Symbol: a vessel with two outlets: water to sea, oil to the sludge tank. [S4]
- Real working: it separates oil from bilge water, mostly by gravity, with a coalescing second stage; a monitor measures the oil content; at 15 ppm or less, overboard discharge is allowed; above, an alarm sounds and a three-way valve sends the water back to a tank. [S18]
- States: `stopped`, `dischargingOverboard`, `recirculating`, plus `hasHighOilContentAlarm`.
- Properties: `isRunning` (boolean, M); `oilContentPpm` (number, ppm, M); `hasHighOilContentAlarm` (boolean, D); `isDischargingOverboard` (boolean, X).
- Model: if `oilContentPpm > 15`, alarm and recirculation [S18]; else discharge; the oil content is a scenario parameter.

## 4. Sea chest and trap (ship equipment catalog)

### Sea chest (prise d'eau de mer)

- Symbol: a rectangular box on the hull line, with a grid. [S3]
- Real working: a recess of the hull that holds the suction reserve of raw water circuits, engine cooling included; removable grids close it; baffles reduce the effect of speed and sea state. [S19]
- States: `clear`, `blocked`.
- Properties: `seaWaterTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `hasBlockageAlarm` (boolean, D); the hull valve is a separate valve object.
- Model: an infinite source at the hydrostatic pressure of its depth. [S14]

### Steam trap (purgeur de vapeur)

- Symbol: a small circle or square on the drain line; a public plant data model has a class for it. [S4] [S46]
- Real working: mechanical (float, bucket), thermostatic (bellows, bimetal) or orifice types; a strainer sits just before each trap. [S15]
- States: `closed`, `discharging`, plus `hasTrapFault`.
- Properties: `isDischarging` (boolean, X); `hasTrapFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: discharges condensate as soon as there is some; stays closed on steam.

## Sources

- [S18] Wikipedia, "Oily water separator (marine)", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oily_water_separator_(marine)>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S19] Wikipedia, "Sea chest (nautical)", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_chest_(nautical)>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S20] Wärtsilä, "Encyclopedia of Marine and Energy Technology, Central Cooling Water System", <https://www.wartsila.com/encyclopedia/term/central-cooling-water-system>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, summarized.
- [S21] Wärtsilä, "Encyclopedia of Marine and Energy Technology, Fuel oil system", <https://www.wartsila.com/encyclopedia/term/fuel-oil-system>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, summarized.

- [S36] Modelica Association, "PumpCharacteristics", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.Machines.BaseClasses.PumpCharacteristics.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Fluid equipment property sets

Contents: 1. Roles · 2. Shared sets · 3. Derived states · Sources

The names follow `docs/synoptics/symbol-property-naming.md`. The catalog cards (`catalog-*.md`) list only what they add to these sets.

## 1. Roles of a property (fluid equipment)

Every property has one role. The public models all keep what is commanded apart from what is measured. [S43] [S44]

| Role | Letter | Example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Parameter of the model | P | `travelTimeS`, `kvM3PerH` |
| Command or setpoint | C | `isOpenCommanded`, `commandedPositionPercent`, `outletPressureSetpointBar` |
| Measured or feedback | M | `isOpen`, `actualPositionPercent`, `flowRateM3PerH` |
| Fault or alarm | D | `hasPositionFault`, `hasHighLevelAlarm` |
| Derived by the model | X | `positionState`, `runState` |

Parameters, commands and setpoints go into the model; it gives the measures and the faults. The symbol shows only measures, faults and derived states.

## 2. The seven shared sets (fluid equipment)

| Set | Properties |
| --- | --- |
| `identity` | `tagName`, `description`, `systemCode` (string, P) [S45] |
| `controlMode` | `controlMode` (enum `auto`, `manual`; M); `controlLocation` (enum `remote`, `local`; M); `isInterlocked` (boolean, M) [S43] |
| `onOffValve` | `isOpenCommanded` (boolean, C); `isOpen`, `isClosed` (boolean, M, limit switches); `positionState` (enum, X); `hasPositionFault` (boolean, D); `travelTimeS` (number, s, P); `failPosition` (enum `open`, `closed`, `inPlace`; P) [S43] [S30] [S28] |
| `modulatingValve` | `commandedPositionPercent` (number, %, C); `actualPositionPercent` (number, %, M); `hasPositionFault` (boolean, D); `kvM3PerH` (number, m³/h, P); `travelTimeS` (number, s, P); `failPosition` (enum, P) [S43] [S28] [S33] |
| `drive` | `isRunCommanded` (boolean, C); `isRunning` (boolean, M); `commandedSpeedRpm`, `actualSpeedRpm` (number, rpm, C and M); `runState` (enum, X); `hasTripFault`, `hasStartFault` (boolean, D); `startTimeS` (number, s, P) [S43] |
| `tank` | `levelPercent` (number, %, M); `levelM` (number, m, M); `volumeM3` (number, m³, X); `capacityM3` (number, m³, P); `temperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `hasHighLevelAlarm`, `hasLowLevelAlarm` (boolean, D) [S11] [S37] |
| `processPoint` | `inletPressureBar`, `outletPressureBar`, `differentialPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `flowRateM3PerH` (number, m³/h, M); `temperatureDegC` (number, °C, M) [S45] [S44] |

Every symbol also carries the common inputs of the naming document: `health`, `quality`, alarm priority and state.

A motorized valve gives way to its position when power fails; a spring-return valve goes to its fail position: hence `failPosition`. [S30]

## 3. Derived states (valve and drive)

These rules derive the states from the commands and the feedbacks; they follow the monitoring of a public data model. [S43]

- `positionState`: only closed feedback true, `closed`; only open feedback true, `open`; no feedback and an open order, `opening`; no feedback and a close order, `closing`; both feedbacks true, `unknown` with `hasPositionFault`.
- `hasPositionFault`: the feedback does not match the order after `travelTimeS`.
- `runState`: run order without feedback, `starting`; run feedback, `running`; order removed with feedback still true, `stopping`; no order and no feedback, `stopped`. A trip sets `hasTripFault`.
- `hasStartFault`: no run feedback after `startTimeS`.
- A hand valve without sensor has no order and no feedback: it keeps only `isOpen`, set by the operator or the instructor.

## Sources

- [S11] US Navy, "Fluid Power", NAVEDTRA 14105A, chapter 9, <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-reservoirs-strainers-filters-accumulators-navy>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S28] Wikipedia, "Control valve", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Control_valve>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S30] Wikipedia, "Valve actuator", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valve_actuator>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S33] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid User's Guide, Valve characteristics", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.UsersGuide.ComponentDefinition.ValveCharacteristics.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S37] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid.Vessels.OpenTank", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.Vessels.OpenTank.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S43] Beckhoff Automation, "TF8400 TwinCAT 3 MTP Runtime", version 1.1.1, <https://download.beckhoff.com/download/Document/automation/twincat3/TF8400_TC3_MTP_Runtime_EN.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, summarized.
- [S44] Project Haystack, "Points", <https://project-haystack.org/doc/docHaystack/Points>, read 2026-11-12, AFL 3.0, summarized.
- [S45] OPC Foundation, "OPC 40223, OPC UA for Pumps and Vacuum Pumps", section 1, <https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-40223/1>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

id: synoptics-fluid-model-laws
title: Fluid model laws and levels of detail
topic: synoptics
summary: Four laws and two relations that animate a fluid synoptic, and the three levels of model detail to choose from.
tool: none
version: "none"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S10, S14, S33, S34, S35, S36, S37, S38, S39, S40, S41, S42]
license: MIT (own words; standards are cited by reference, never copied)
variant: chat

# Fluid model laws and levels of detail

Contents: 1. Laws · 2. Levels of detail · 3. Keep it simple · Sources

## 1. Four laws and two relations (fluid model)

| Law | Form | Use | Limits |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Valve flow coefficient | `Q = Kv × √(Δp / (ρ / ρ0))`, `Q` in m³/h, `Δp` in bar, `ρ0` density of water. `Kv`: water flow in m³/h under 1 bar | Any valve or orifice [S33] [S38] | Liquids. For a gas, only while the pressure drop stays under 0.2 times the inlet absolute pressure; beyond, choked flow models. Not for non-Newtonian fluids or slurries [S34] [S42] |
| Pump curve and affinity | Head-flow curve at rated speed, linear, quadratic or polynomial; at another speed, flow scales with speed, head with its square, power with its cube | Centrifugal pumps, fans [S35] [S36] [S40] | Similar machines, efficiency taken constant. A positive displacement pump gives a flow proportional to speed, whatever the pressure [S10] |
| Tank volume balance | `dV/dt = inflows − outflows`; `level = V / area`; bottom pressure = height × specific weight | Tanks, sumps, ballast tanks [S14] [S37] | Constant cross-section; stop the computation when the tank overflows |
| Pressure loss | `Δp = f × (L / D) × ρ × v² / 2`; turbulent: loss grows as the square of the speed; laminar: `f = 64 / Re` | Pipes, filters, exchangers [S41] | For a synoptic: `Δp = K × Q²`, one coefficient per branch |
| Cylinder force | `F = p × A`; rod side: piston area − rod area | Cylinders [S14] | Friction ignored |
| Speed and flow | `Q = v × A` | Cylinders, pipes [S14] | Incompressible fluid |

- `Cv` is the water flow in US gallons per minute under 1 psi at 60 °F. Use one factor in the whole project: `Cv = 1.16 × Kv`. [S39]
- Control valve sizing follows IEC 60534-2-1; its liquid equations assume an incompressible Newtonian fluid. [S42]
- Opening dynamics: filter the opening order with a rise time, or move on a ramp, to avoid unrealistic flow reversals. [S34]
- Gas volumes (air receiver, accumulator): ideal gas law and gas compression (elementary physics, analysis).

## 2. Three levels of model detail (fluid synoptic)

| Level | What it computes | Fits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0. Logic | States only. A line is "fed" when a path of open valves links it to a running source | Colouring pipes, checking a valve line-up, procedure training [S34] |
| 1. Quasi-static | One flow and one pressure per branch: valve law, pump curve, `Δp = K × Q²`; levels integrated in time | Credible flows, pressures and levels; alarms and trips [S33] [S35] [S41] |
| 2. Dynamic | Level 1 plus opening delays, temperatures, gases, start-ups | Control loops, transients, fine faults [S34] |

Choose level 1 by default, level 0 for circuits without a shown measure, level 2 only for the part a scenario plays.

## 3. Keep the model simple (fluid synoptic)

- One coefficient per branch.
- No implicit equation when an explicit rule is enough: `outlet pressure = min(inlet pressure, setpoint)`.
- Every fault parameter is a named property: `cloggingPercent`, `failPosition`.
- The simplest public valve model, with a pressure loss proportional to flow, is presented by its documentation as a test model: say so when you use it. [S34]

## Sources

- [S10] US Navy, "Fluid Power", NAVEDTRA 14105A, chapter 4 "Pumps", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-pumps-navy>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S14] Same, chapter 2 "Forces in Liquids", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulics-forces-in-liquids-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S33] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid User's Guide, Valve characteristics", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.UsersGuide.ComponentDefinition.ValveCharacteristics.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S34] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid.Valves", Modelica Standard Library 4.0.0, <https://doc.modelica.org/Modelica%204.0.0/Resources/helpDymola/Modelica_Fluid_Valves.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S35] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid.Machines.BaseClasses.PartialPump", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.Machines.BaseClasses.PartialPump.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S36] Modelica Association, "PumpCharacteristics", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.Machines.BaseClasses.PumpCharacteristics.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

---
id: synoptics-symbol-component
title: Symbol component
topic: synoptics
summary: How to code a reusable SVG symbol, with typed inputs, data attributes, CSS variables and one flash clock.
tool: none

checked: 2026-11-12
sources: [S2, S14, S32, S33, S34]

---

# Symbol component

Contents: 1. Structure · 2. Inputs and view function · 3. Markup · 4. Styles · 5. Flash clock · 6. Pitfalls · 7. Final check · Sources

## 1. Structure (symbol component)

- One symbol is one pure function: the same inputs always give the same picture.
- The symbol reads no data by itself. It starts no timer. It keeps no state.
- The inputs carry states and values. They never carry a colour.
- The function turns the inputs into `data-*` attributes, one state word and one accessible name.
- The styles choose the fill, the pattern and the badge from the `data-*` attributes.
- Every colour comes from a CSS variable set on the view.
- Follow the document "Symbol property naming" for every input name.

## 2. Inputs and view function (symbol component)

```ts
export type PositionState = 'open' | 'closed' | 'opening' | 'closing' | 'intermediate' | 'unknown';
export type AlarmPriority = 'none' | 'caution' | 'warning' | 'alarm' | 'emergency';
export type AlarmState = 'normal' | 'unacknowledged' | 'acknowledged';

export interface ValveSymbolInputs {
  readonly tagName: string;
  readonly positionState: PositionState;
  readonly actualPositionPercent?: number;
  readonly alarmPriority: AlarmPriority;
  readonly alarmState: AlarmState;
  readonly isOutOfService: boolean;
}

export interface SymbolView {
  readonly attributes: Readonly<Record<string, string>>;
  readonly stateText: string;
  readonly badgeLetter: string;
  readonly isBadgeFlashing: boolean;
  readonly accessibleName: string;
}

const POSITION_TEXT: Readonly<Record<PositionState, string>> = {
  open: 'OPEN',
  closed: 'CLOSED',
  opening: 'OPENING',
  closing: 'CLOSING',
  intermediate: 'PARTIAL',
  unknown: '?',
};

const BADGE_LETTER: Readonly<Record<AlarmPriority, string>> = {
  none: '',
  caution: 'C',
  warning: 'W',
  alarm: 'A',
  emergency: 'E',
};

export function toValveView(inputs: ValveSymbolInputs): SymbolView {
  const stateText =
    inputs.positionState === 'intermediate' && inputs.actualPositionPercent !== undefined
      ? `${String(Math.round(inputs.actualPositionPercent))} %`
      : POSITION_TEXT[inputs.positionState];
  const hasAlert = inputs.alarmPriority !== 'none';
  const alarmText = hasAlert ? `, ${inputs.alarmPriority} ${inputs.alarmState}` : '';

  return {
    attributes: {
      'data-position': inputs.positionState,
      'data-alarm-priority': inputs.alarmPriority,
      'data-alarm-state': inputs.alarmState,
      'data-out-of-service': String(inputs.isOutOfService),
    },
    stateText,
    badgeLetter: BADGE_LETTER[inputs.alarmPriority],
    isBadgeFlashing: hasAlert && inputs.alarmState === 'unacknowledged',
    accessibleName: `Valve ${inputs.tagName}, ${stateText}${alarmText}`,
  };
}
```

- Write one such function per symbol type. Test it without a browser: give inputs, compare the result.
- Copy the template `templates/typescript-angular/synoptic-symbol.ts`; it passes the lint of the base.
- Use a `Record` keyed by the state type. The compiler then refuses a missing state.

## 3. Markup (symbol component)

Draw the symbol inline, in one `svg` element. Put the attributes of the view function on the root.

```html
<svg class="syn-symbol syn-valve" viewBox="0 0 64 80" role="img"
     aria-label="Valve V101, OPEN, warning unacknowledged"
     data-position="open" data-alarm-priority="warning" data-alarm-state="unacknowledged"
     data-out-of-service="false">
  <path class="syn-shape" d="M8 16 L32 32 L8 48 Z M56 16 L32 32 L56 48 Z" />
  <g class="syn-badge" transform="translate(44 0)">
    <path class="syn-badge-shape" d="M9 1 L17 17 L1 17 Z" />
    <text class="syn-badge-letter" x="9" y="15" text-anchor="middle">W</text>
  </g>
  <text class="syn-state" x="32" y="62" text-anchor="middle">OPEN</text>
  <text class="syn-tag" x="32" y="76" text-anchor="middle">V101</text>
</svg>
```

- Give every symbol type the same `viewBox` grid, so that symbols line up.
- Keep the same parts in the same order: shape, badge, state word, tag.
- Set `role="img"` and `aria-label` with the accessible name: type, tag, state word, alert. [S14]
- To rotate a symbol, rotate the shape only. Keep the texts horizontal. [S2]

Define the hatch pattern once per page:

```html
<svg width="0" height="0" aria-hidden="true">
  <defs>
    <pattern id="syn-hatch" width="6" height="6" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse" patternTransform="rotate(45)">
      <line class="syn-hatch-line" x1="0" y1="0" x2="0" y2="6" />
    </pattern>
  </defs>
</svg>
```

## 4. Styles (symbol component)

```css
.syn-view {
  --syn-bg: #d9d9d9;
  --syn-line: #333333;
  --syn-text: #1a1a1a;
  --syn-fill-active: #ffffff;
  --syn-fill-inactive: #595959;
  --syn-alert-alarm: #c4001a;
  --syn-alert-warning: #e07000;
  --syn-alert-caution: #f2d000;
  --syn-invalid: #7a1fa2;
  background: var(--syn-bg);
}
.syn-view[data-palette="night"] {
  --syn-bg: #1a1a1a;
  --syn-line: #b3b3b3;
  --syn-text: #e6e6e6;
  --syn-fill-active: #e6e6e6;
  --syn-fill-inactive: #6b6b6b;
  --syn-alert-alarm: #ff4d4d;
  --syn-alert-warning: #ff9a1f;
  --syn-alert-caution: #ffe14d;
  --syn-invalid: #d48cff;
}
.syn-shape { fill: var(--syn-fill-inactive); stroke: var(--syn-line); stroke-width: 2; }
.syn-hatch-line { stroke: var(--syn-line); stroke-width: 2; }
.syn-symbol[data-position="open"] .syn-shape,
.syn-symbol[data-position="intermediate"] .syn-shape { fill: var(--syn-fill-active); }
.syn-symbol[data-position="opening"] .syn-shape,
.syn-symbol[data-position="closing"] .syn-shape { fill: url(#syn-hatch); }
.syn-symbol[data-position="unknown"] .syn-shape { fill: none; stroke: var(--syn-invalid); stroke-dasharray: 4 3; }
.syn-state, .syn-tag { fill: var(--syn-text); font: 10px sans-serif; }
.syn-badge { display: none; }
.syn-symbol:not([data-alarm-priority="none"]) .syn-badge { display: inline; }
.syn-badge-shape { stroke: var(--syn-line); stroke-width: 1.5; }
.syn-symbol[data-alarm-priority="caution"] .syn-badge-shape { fill: var(--syn-alert-caution); }
.syn-symbol[data-alarm-priority="warning"] .syn-badge-shape { fill: var(--syn-alert-warning); }
.syn-symbol[data-alarm-priority="alarm"] .syn-badge-shape,
.syn-symbol[data-alarm-priority="emergency"] .syn-badge-shape { fill: var(--syn-alert-alarm); }
.syn-view[data-flash-phase="off"] .syn-symbol[data-alarm-state="unacknowledged"] .syn-badge { opacity: 0.25; }
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .syn-symbol[data-alarm-state="unacknowledged"] .syn-badge-shape { stroke-width: 4; }
}
```

- The closed valve keeps the dark default fill. The open valve takes the bright fill.
- The badge always has a border in the line colour.
- The night palette changes only the variables. No symbol changes.
- With reduced motion, the badge does not flash. Its border is thicker instead. [S14] [S32]

## 5. Flash clock (symbol component)

Start one clock per view. It sets `data-flash-phase` on the view root. All badges then flash in phase, at 1 Hz.

```ts
export function startFlashClock(root: HTMLElement, periodMs = 1000): () => void {
  if (window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches) {
    root.dataset['flashPhase'] = 'on';
    return () => undefined;
  }
  let isOn = true;
  const timer = window.setInterval(() => {
    isOn = !isOn;
    root.dataset['flashPhase'] = isOn ? 'on' : 'off';
  }, periodMs / 2);
  return () => window.clearInterval(timer);
}
```

- Call the returned function when the view closes. It stops the clock.
- Never write a CSS animation inside a symbol. Two symbols would then flash out of phase.

## 6. Pitfalls (SVG symbol and use elements)

- A `symbol` element is never drawn by itself. Only a `use` element that points to it is drawn. [S34]
- A `use` element copies the shape into a tree that the page cannot reach. A style rule of the page does not select the parts of the copy. Only inherited properties get in. [S33]
- An attribute set inside the copied shape wins over the same attribute set on `use`. A `fill` on `use` has no effect when the shape already has a `fill`. [S33]
- `width` and `height` on `use` work only when the target is an `svg` or a `symbol` with a `viewBox`. [S33]
- A browser can refuse a `use` that points to a file from another origin. [S33]
- So: use `symbol` and `use` only for a part that never changes with the state. Draw every part that changes inline, as in section 3.
- The hatch does not show: check that the pattern with the id `syn-hatch` is in the same page.
- A graphic part needs a contrast of at least 3:1 with what touches it. Check each new colour in both palettes. [S14]

## 7. Final check (symbol component)

- Does the symbol take states and values only, and no colour?
- Is every colour a CSS variable?
- Is the state shown by a fill or a shape, and by a word?
- Does only the badge of an unacknowledged alarm or warning flash, from the view clock?
- Does the root have `role="img"` and an `aria-label` with the tag and the state word?

## Sources

- [S2] U.S. NRC, "Human-System Interface Design Review Guidelines", NUREG-0700 Rev. 3, 2020, sections 1.3.3 to 1.3.5 and 1.3.10, <https://www.nrc.gov/docs/ML2016/ML20162A214.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, U.S. federal publication, summarized.
- [S14] W3C, "Web Content Accessibility Guidelines (WCAG) 2.2", Recommendation of 2024-12-12, <https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/>, read 2026-11-12, W3C Document License, summarized.
- [S32] MDN, "prefers-reduced-motion", <https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/At-rules/@media/prefers-reduced-motion>, read 2026-11-12, license not read, summarized.
- [S33] MDN, "use, SVG element", <https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/SVG/Reference/Element/use>, read 2026-11-12, Creative Commons, summarized.
- [S34] MDN, "symbol, SVG element", <https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/SVG/Reference/Element/symbol>, read 2026-11-12, Creative Commons, summarized.

---
id: synoptics-symbol-property-naming
title: Symbol property naming
topic: synoptics
summary: Naming rules for the properties of a synoptic symbol and of its equipment model, with wrong and right examples.
tool: none
version: "none"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S16, S17, S18, S19, S25, S26, S27, S28, S30, S31, S35, S36]

---

# Symbol property naming

Contents: 1. Casing · 2. Units · 3. Booleans · 4. States · 5. Command and feedback · 6. Common properties · 7. Final check · Sources

## 1. Casing (symbol property names)

- Write names in English. Use full words. Use nouns, not verbs.
- In TypeScript and in JSON: `camelCase`.
- In C#: `PascalCase`. [S30]
- Keep the same words on both sides. Let the JSON serializer convert the case. Never rename by hand. [S31]
- Write an acronym as a word: `tagId`, not `tagID`. [S18]

Right, in C#:

```csharp
public enum PositionState { Open, Closed, Opening, Closing, Intermediate, Unknown }

public sealed record ValveState(
    string TagName,
    PositionState PositionState,
    double CommandedPositionPercent,
    double ActualPositionPercent);

var options = new JsonSerializerOptions(JsonSerializerDefaults.Web);
options.Converters.Add(new JsonStringEnumConverter(JsonNamingPolicy.CamelCase));
// JSON: {"tagName":"V101","positionState":"open","commandedPositionPercent":100,"actualPositionPercent":100}
```

## 2. Units (symbol property names)

- A bare number with a physical unit carries its unit as the last word of its name. [S28]
- Take the suffix from the table below. Add a line to the table before you use a new suffix.
- Write the suffix as a word: first letter upper case, the rest lower case.
- Use `Percent` for a value from 0 to 100. Use `Ratio` for a value from 0 to 1.
- Use `Count` for a number of items. [S28]
- When the unit can change at run time, do not use a suffix. Use a value object with a `unit` field instead. Never do both. [S17] [S27] [S28]

| Quantity | Suffix | Example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pressure | `Bar`, `Mbar`, `Kpa` | `dischargePressureBar` |
| Temperature | `DegC` | `oilTemperatureDegC` |
| Temperature rise | `DegCPerMin` | `rateOfRiseThresholdDegCPerMin` |
| Level, opening, load | `Percent` | `tankLevelPercent` |
| Volume flow | `M3PerH`, `LPerMin` | `coolingFlowM3PerH` |
| Rotation speed | `Rpm` | `shaftSpeedRpm` |
| Ship or fluid speed | `Kn`, `MPerS` | `shipSpeedKn` |
| Voltage | `V` | `busVoltageV` |
| Current | `A` | `motorCurrentA` |
| Frequency | `Hz` | `busFrequencyHz` |
| Power | `Kw` | `activePowerKw` |
| Energy | `Kwh` | `suppliedEnergyKwh` |
| Length, depth | `M`, `Mm` | `keelDepthM` |
| Angle | `Deg` | `rudderAngleDeg` |
| Duration | `S`, `Ms` | `travelTimeS` |
| Mass | `Kg` | `fuelMassKg` |
| Volume | `M3`, `L` | `tankVolumeM3` |
| Small current | `Ma` | `loopCurrentMa` |
| Reactive and apparent power | `Kvar`, `Kva` | `reactivePowerKvar` |
| Battery charge | `Ah` | `batteryChargeAh` |
| Turn rate | `DegPerS` | `rateOfTurnDegPerS` |
| Torque | `Nm`, `Knm` | `shaftTorqueKnm` |
| Force | `N` | `rodForceN` |
| Run time | `H` | `runTimeH` |
| Inertia | `KgM2` | `shaftInertiaKgM2` |
| Ratio, count | `Ratio`, `Count` | `gearRatio`, `startCount` |
| Displacement, area | `Cm3`, `Cm2`, `M2` | `displacementCm3`, `pistonAreaCm2` |
| Low pressure | `Pa` | `filterDifferentialPressurePa` |
| Concentration | `Ppm` | `oilContentPpm` |
| Density, mass flow | `KgPerM3`, `KgPerS` | `densityKgPerM3`, `massFlowRateKgPerS` |
| Kinematic viscosity | `Cst` | `fuelViscosityCst` |

Write each unit with one spelling only: `Percent`, never `Pct`; `DegC`, never `C`; `M3PerH`, never `M3h`.

Wrong:

```ts
export interface PumpWrong {
  Pressure: number;
  pos: number;
  target: number;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export interface PumpRight {
  readonly dischargePressureBar: number;
  readonly dischargePressureSetpointBar: number;
  readonly shaftSpeedRpm: number;
  readonly startCount: number;
}
```

Right, when the unit can change:

```ts
export interface Measurement {
  readonly value: number;
  readonly unit: string;
}

export interface TankRight {
  readonly level: Measurement;
}
```

## 3. Booleans (symbol property names)

- Start a boolean with `is`, `has` or `can`. [S30]
- Name the true case in the affirmative: `isReady`, never `isNotReady`. [S30]
- Use a boolean only for a fact that stands alone.
- Never use two booleans for states that exclude each other. Use one state property (section 4).

Wrong:

```ts
export interface ValveWrong {
  open: boolean;
  closed: boolean;
  notReady: boolean;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export interface ValveRight {
  readonly positionState: 'open' | 'closed' | 'opening' | 'closing' | 'intermediate' | 'unknown';
  readonly isReady: boolean;
  readonly hasPositionFeedback: boolean;
}
```

## 4. States (symbol property names)

- Give one property to each group of states that exclude each other. [S16] [S19]
- End its name with `State`, `Mode`, `Location`, `Priority` or with the plain noun (`health`, `quality`).
- Write the values as `camelCase` words: `'maintenanceRequired'`.
- A switching device has a position with four values, as in IEC 61850: `intermediate` while it moves, `bad` when the two contacts disagree. [S36]
- Always include a value for the unknown case when the state comes from a sensor.

| Property | Values |
| --- | --- |
| `positionState` | `open`, `closed`, `opening`, `closing`, `intermediate`, `unknown` |
| `runState` | `running`, `stopped`, `starting`, `stopping`, `unknown` |
| `controlLocation` | `remote`, `local` |
| `controlMode` | `auto`, `manual` |
| `switchPosition` | `intermediate`, `open`, `closed`, `bad` (circuit breakers, switches, disconnectors) |
| `health` | `normal`, `failure`, `checkFunction`, `offSpec`, `maintenanceRequired` |
| `quality` | `good`, `uncertain`, `bad` |
| `alarmPriority` | `none`, `caution`, `warning`, `alarm`, `emergency` |
| `alarmState` | `normal`, `unacknowledged`, `acknowledged`, `returnedUnacknowledged`, `shelved`, `suppressed`, `outOfService` |

## 5. Command and feedback (symbol property names)

- Name what the system asks with `commanded`. Name what the equipment reports with `actual`. [S19]
- Use this pair for a position or a state: `commandedPositionPercent`, `actualPositionPercent`.
- For a regulated quantity, name the target with `Setpoint` before the unit. Name the measured value with the plain quantity.
- For alarm limits, use the four names of OPC UA before the unit: `HighHighLimit`, `HighLimit`, `LowLimit`, `LowLowLimit`; and `alarmDeadband` for the dead band. [S35]
- A symbol with one measured quantity can drop the quantity: `highLimitBar`.
- Order the words like this: location or role, quantity, qualifier, unit.

| Meaning | Name |
| --- | --- |
| Measured pressure at the discharge | `dischargePressureBar` |
| Pressure target | `dischargePressureSetpointBar` |
| High pressure limit | `dischargePressureHighLimitBar` |
| Very high pressure limit | `dischargePressureHighHighLimitBar` |
| Dead band of the alarms | `alarmDeadbandBar` |
| Position asked by the command | `commandedPositionPercent` |
| Position reported by the equipment | `actualPositionPercent` |
| State asked by the command | `commandedRunState` |
| State reported by the equipment | `runState` |

## 6. Common properties (every symbol)

Give these properties to every symbol. Add the properties of the equipment after them.

```ts
export interface CommonSymbolInputs {
  readonly tagName: string;
  readonly description?: string;
  readonly health: 'normal' | 'failure' | 'checkFunction' | 'offSpec' | 'maintenanceRequired';
  readonly quality: 'good' | 'uncertain' | 'bad';
  readonly controlLocation: 'remote' | 'local';
  readonly controlMode: 'auto' | 'manual';
  readonly isOutOfService: boolean;
  readonly isInhibited: boolean;
  readonly isSimulated: boolean;
  readonly alarmPriority: 'none' | 'caution' | 'warning' | 'alarm' | 'emergency';
  readonly alarmState:
    | 'normal'
    | 'unacknowledged'
    | 'acknowledged'
    | 'returnedUnacknowledged'
    | 'shelved'
    | 'suppressed'
    | 'outOfService';
}
```

- `tagName` is the identifier shown on the view. Show it as given. Never build it and never parse it. [S25] [S26]
- `health` follows the four standard diagnostic cases plus `normal`. [S16]

## 7. Final check (symbol property names)

- Does every bare number with a unit end with a suffix from the table?
- Does every boolean start with `is`, `has` or `can`, in the affirmative?
- Is every group of exclusive states one property, never several booleans?
- Are the words the same in TypeScript and in C#, with only the case changed?

## Sources

- [S16] OPC Foundation, "OPC 10000-100: Devices", v1.05, section 4.5.4, <https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/4.5.4>, read 2026-11-12, OPC Foundation license, summarized.
- [S17] OPC Foundation, "OPC 10000-8: Data Access", v1.05.07, <https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-8/full>, read 2026-11-12, OPC Foundation license, summarized.
- [S18] OPC Foundation, "OPC 11030: UA Modelling Best Practices", section 2.1.2, <https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-11030/2.1.2>, read 2026-11-12, OPC Foundation license, summarized.
- [S19] DNV, "DNV naming rule" (ISO 19848 Local ID), <https://docs.vista.dnv.com/docs/standards/dnv-naming-rule>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S25] IEC, "IEC CDD, IEC 61987, property tag name", <https://cdd.iec.ch/cdd/iec61987/iec61987.nsf/0/1BE8325198445094C1258A7D0039DA99>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, summarized.
- [S26] POSC Caesar Association, "CFIHOS RDL v2.0, property name", <https://posccaesar.org/cfihos/v2.0/CFIHOS-10000056>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S27] Microsoft, "DTDL v4, QuantitativeTypes extension v2", <https://azure.github.io/opendigitaltwins-dtdl/DTDL/v4/DTDL.quantitativeTypes.v2.html>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S28] Google, "AIP-141: Quantities", <https://google.aip.dev/141>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S30] Microsoft, "Names of Type Members", Framework Design Guidelines, <https://learn.microsoft.com/sl-si/dotnet/standard/design-guidelines/names-of-type-members>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, summarized.

- [S36] ABB, "IEC 61850 server, Introduction", <https://help.plc.abb.com/iec_61850_server_introduction.html>, read 2026-11-12, manufacturer source, license not stated, summarized.

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== FILE 1/1 9102a3f1597041e887ee9adcb6c640a8991a25820e64848d4841de2c02379259 RAW chat/knowledge/synoptics/synoptic-rules.md
---
id: synoptics-synoptic-rules
title: Synoptic display rules
topic: synoptics
summary: Rules to lay out a synoptic view and to show equipment states, alerts and flashing.
tool: none
version: "none"
checked: 2026-11-12
sources: [S1, S2, S4, S5, S6, S11, S12, S14, S16, S32]
license: MIT (own words; standards and guides are cited by reference, never copied)
variant: chat
---

# Synoptic display rules

Contents: 1. Layout · 2. Colour · 3. Equipment states · 4. Alerts · 5. Flashing and motion · 6. Text and numbers · 7. Final check · Sources

## 1. Layout (synoptic view)

- Show only what the operator's task needs. Draw no decoration, no 3D effect, no logo. [S2]
- Label every component. Start and end every flow line at a label or at a labelled component. [S2]
- Mark the flow direction with arrowheads. [S2]
- Do not overlap lines. Draw each crossing so that it cannot be read as a junction. [S2]
- Make what changes (a valve position, a value) stand out more than what never changes (a pipe, a tank outline). [S2]
- Put each value next to the equipment it belongs to. [S2]
- Give every view a short title at the top. [S2]
- Keep the view sparse. Use at most half of the area. Use at most a quarter on a view that is mostly text. [S2]
- Keep the same place for the same thing on every view. [S6]
- Build four levels of views and link them: overview, system, equipment detail, diagnostic. Put no command on the overview. [S1]

## 2. Colour (synoptic view)

- Use a plain background with no pattern. [S2]
- Provide a day palette and a night palette. At night, draw light items on a dark background. [S6]
- Take every colour from a CSS variable. Never write a colour value inside a symbol.
- Use few colours. Give one meaning to one colour. [S2]
- Draw equipment, pipes and normal values in neutral greys. Use bright colour only for an abnormal situation. [S1]
- Reserve red, orange and yellow for alerts and for invalid data. [S5] [S6]
- Never use red or green to mean open or closed, running or stopped. People from different trades read them in opposite ways. [S2]
- Never use colour alone. Add a shape, a pattern or a word that gives the same information. [S2] [S6] [S14]
- Keep a contrast of at least 3:1 between a graphic part and what touches it. Keep at least 4.5:1 for text. [S14]
- In a normal situation, highlight at most about one tenth of the view. [S2]

Wrong:

```css
.syn-valve[data-position="open"] .syn-shape { fill: green; }
.syn-valve[data-position="closed"] .syn-shape { fill: red; }
```

Right:

```css
.syn-valve[data-position="open"] .syn-shape { fill: var(--syn-fill-active); }
.syn-valve[data-position="closed"] .syn-shape { fill: var(--syn-fill-inactive); }
/* The symbol also shows the word OPEN or CLOSED. */
```

## 3. Equipment states (synoptic symbol)

Show each state with a fill or a shape, and with a word. Use this table.

| State | Fill or shape | Word or badge |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Open, running, on | Bright fill | `OPEN`, `RUN`, `ON` |
| Closed, stopped, off | Dark fill | `CLOSED`, `STOP`, `OFF` |
| Opening, closing, starting, stopping | Hatched fill | `OPENING`, `CLOSING`, `STARTING`, `STOPPING` |
| Partly open | Bright fill | The position, for example `45 %` |
| Unknown position, invalid value | No fill, dashed outline | `?` |
| Fault, or position different from the command | Alert badge (section 4) | The alert text |
| Local control | Normal fill | Badge `LOC` |
| Manual mode | Normal fill | Badge `MAN` |
| Out of service, locked out | Cross-hatch over the symbol | Badge `OOS` |
| Inhibited, interlocked | Normal fill | Badge `INH` |
| Simulated or forced value | Normal fill | Badge `SIM` |
| Device diagnostic | Normal fill | Badge `FAIL`, `CHECK`, `OFFSPEC` or `MAINT` |

- Use only two brightness levels: bright and dark. [S2]
- Show a mode badge only when the mode is not the normal mode of the equipment.
- Use the four standard diagnostic cases: failure, function check, out of specification, maintenance required. A forced or substitute value is a function check. Local operation is a function check. [S16]
- Never show an invalid or doubtful value as a good value. Mark it. [S6]
- Write the words in the language of the project screens. The words above are the English defaults.

## 4. Alerts (synoptic symbol and alert list)

Use four priorities. [S4] [S5]

| Priority | Meaning | Not acknowledged | Acknowledged | Sound |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `emergency` | Immediate danger to life or to the ship | Flashing | Steady | Follow the emergency signal rules of the project |
| `alarm` | Needs attention and action now | Flashing | Steady until the cause is gone | Until acknowledged |
| `warning` | Needs attention now, no action yet | Flashing | Steady until the cause is gone | One short signal |
| `caution` | Needs more attention than usual | Steady | Steady | None |

- Show an alert with a badge next to the equipment that raised it. [S1]
- Give each priority its own outer shape, its own letter and its own colour. Defaults: `E` in an octagon, red; `A` in a square, red; `W` in a triangle, orange; `C` in a circle, yellow. [S1] [S2]
- Draw a dark border around every badge. Orange and yellow do not contrast enough with a light background.
- Use the alert colours and the badge shapes for alerts only. [S1] [S2]
- Acknowledge alerts one by one. Never acknowledge a group in one action. [S4]
- For a shelved, suppressed or out-of-service alarm: show no alert colour and no flashing. A small neutral marker is allowed. Provide a list of these alarms. [S11] [S12]
- In the alert list: show at least 20 alerts at once. Sort by priority, then by time. Keep a history with the times raised, acknowledged and cleared. [S4]
- From an alert, open the view of its source in one action. [S11]

## 5. Flashing and motion (synoptic symbol)

- Flash only an alarm or a warning that is not acknowledged. Flash nothing else. [S4] [S6]
- Use one rate: 1 Hz, bright for half of the period. Stay between 0.5 Hz and 1.5 Hz. [S5]
- Never flash more than three times per second. [S14]
- Flash the badge only. Never flash a text that must be read. Never flash the whole symbol. [S2]
- Flash between two visible levels. Never hide the item. [S2]
- Flash all the badges of a view in phase, from one clock.
- When the user asks for reduced motion, stop the flashing and draw a thick border instead. [S14] [S32]
- Add no other animation: no rotating pump, no moving fluid, no growing bar. [S2]

## 6. Text and numbers (synoptic symbol)

- Write every label and every number horizontally, even when the symbol is rotated. [S2]
- Put the label close to its symbol. [S2]
- Use the same label for the same item on every view. [S2]
- Spell words out. Abbreviate only when space forces it. Never use one abbreviation for two words. [S2]
- Show each value with its unit. Use the same unit for the same quantity on every view. [S6]
- Show only the digits the task needs. [S2]
- Refresh a value that must be read at most once per second. [S2]
- Add an arrow when the operator must see the direction of change. [S2]

## 7. Final check (synoptic view)

- Is every colour information doubled by a shape, a pattern or a word?
- Is red or green used for open, closed, running or stopped? It must not be.
- Does anything flash other than an unacknowledged alarm or warning? It must not.
- Does every colour come from a CSS variable?
- Does every flow line start and end at a label or at a labelled component?

## Sources

- [S1] B. R. Hollifield, "The High Performance HMI", ISA Interchange, undated, <https://blog.isa.org/the-high-performance-hmi>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S2] U.S. NRC, "Human-System Interface Design Review Guidelines", NUREG-0700 Rev. 3, 2020, sections 1.2.8, 1.3.2 to 1.3.10, 1.4, 1.5, <https://www.nrc.gov/docs/ML2016/ML20162A214.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, U.S. federal publication, summarized.

# FMI, SSP and co-simulation

Contents: 1. FMI · 2. SSP · 3. Co-simulation · 4. When to use · Sources

## 1. FMI 3.0 (Functional Mock-up Interface)

- An FMU is a ZIP archive: the XML description (`modelDescription.xml`), compiled libraries or C sources, and data. [S1]
- Three interface types: Model Exchange (the solver is in the importer), Co-Simulation (the model usually brings its solver), Scheduled Execution (an outer scheduler activates partitions of the model, for example on a real-time platform). [S1]
- Variables have a causality (parameter, input, output, local, independent) and a variability: see `docs/simulation/component-models.md`. [S1]
- It exchanges a "black box" model between tools and protects intellectual property; it was designed for continuous systems. [S1] [S3]

## 2. SSP 2.0.1 (System Structure and Parameterization)

A tool-neutral format to describe, package and exchange the structure of a system of components, FMUs among them, and its parameter sets. [S2] [S4]

## 3. Co-simulation (orchestration and pitfalls)

- An orchestrator advances the time of each unit and carries outputs to inputs. [S3]
- Jacobi: all units exchange at the same instant and advance in parallel. Gauss-Seidel: they advance in a set order, more accurate, less parallel. [S3]
- Pitfalls: algebraic loops, extrapolation of inputs between two exchanges, no rollback for a black box. [S3]
- Maritime: the Open Simulation Platform (DNV, Kongsberg Maritime, SINTEF Ocean, NTNU) co-simulates independent models with FMI, can describe the structure with SSP, and adds its own interface specification (variable groups, unit conversion, checks before the run). [S5]

## 4. When to use FMI and SSP

| Need | Choice |
| --- | --- |
| Receive or deliver the model of a piece of equipment without its code | FMI |
| Describe the assembly of several FMUs and their parameters | SSP |
| Models written in the same simulator code | Neither: the model interface of the simulator |

## Sources

- [S1] Modelica Association, "Functional Mock-up Interface Specification", version 3.0, 2022-05-10, <https://fmi-standard.org/docs/3.0>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Modelica Association Project SSP, "System Structure and Parameterization", version 2.0.1, <https://ssp-standard.org/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] Gomes, Thule, Broman, Larsen, Vangheluwe, "Co-simulation: State of the art", arXiv:1702.00686, 2017, <https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/1702.00686>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] Modelica Association Project SSP, "ssp-standard", repository, <https://github.com/modelica/ssp-standard>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA 4.0, summarized.
- [S5] Open Simulation Platform, "OSP on GitHub", <https://open-simulation-platform.github.io/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

id: simulation-component-models
title: Component model interface
topic: simulation
summary: Model interface with parameters, inputs, outputs, state, step, save, restore and faults, with a C# template.
