---

# Angular 22: what changed

Contents: 1. Requirements · 2. OnPush by default · 3. Removed and changed · 4. New stable APIs · 5. Deprecated · 6. Migrations · Old patterns · Sources

Use this document only when `@angular/core` in `package.json` is 22 or higher.

## 1. Requirements (Angular 22 versions)

- Node.js: `^22.22.3 || ^24.15.0 || ^26.0.0`. Node.js 20 is not supported. [S2] [S5]
- TypeScript: `>=6.0.0 <6.1.0`. TypeScript 5.9 and TypeScript 7 are outside this range: never move `typescript` to 7. [S2]
- RxJS: `^6.5.3 || ^7.4.0`. [S2]
- Released on 2026-06-03. Active support ends in 2027-06, long-term support in 2028-06. [S1]

## 2. OnPush by default (Angular 22 change detection)

A component without `changeDetection` uses OnPush. `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Default` is renamed `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Eager`. [S4]

- Do not write `changeDetection: ChangeDetectionStrategy.OnPush`. [S3]
- Keep component state in signals, so that the view updates.
- Keep `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Eager` where the update added it. Remove it only when the component reads its state from signals.

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-clock', template: '<time>{{ now }}</time>' })
export class Clock {
  public now = new Date();

  public constructor() {
    // With OnPush, the view does not update.
    setInterval(() => (this.now = new Date()), 1000);
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-clock', template: '<time>{{ now() }}</time>' })
export class Clock {
  protected readonly now = signal(new Date());

  public constructor() {
    const timer = setInterval(() => this.now.set(new Date()), 1000);

    inject(DestroyRef).onDestroy(() => clearInterval(timer));
  }
}
```

## 3. Removed and changed (Angular 22 breaking changes)

| Old pattern | Write instead |
| --- | --- |
| `ComponentFactoryResolver`, `ComponentFactory` | Pass the component class to `ViewContainerRef.createComponent()` |
| `createNgModuleRef()` | `createNgModule()` |
| `provideRoutes()` | `provideRouter()`, or the `ROUTES` multi token |
| `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Default` | `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Eager` |
| Builders `@angular-devkit/build-angular:jest` and `:web-test-runner` | Builder `@angular/build:unit-test` |

Behaviour that changed: [S4] [S5]

- Router: `paramsInheritanceStrategy` defaults to `'always'`. `CanMatchFn` requires the `currentSnapshot` parameter.
- Forms: the `min` and `max` validation rules accept a number or `null`, not a string.
- Templates: an attribute with the `data-` prefix no longer binds an input or an output. Binding the same input or output twice is a compile error.
- HTTP: `HttpClient` uses the fetch backend by default. To report upload progress, keep the XHR backend with the `withXhr` feature of `provideHttpClient()`.
- Server rendering: a request with an unknown `Host` header gets a 400 response. List the valid hosts in `allowedHosts`.

## 4. New stable APIs (Angular 22: use these in new code)

| Need | Write | Import from |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Singleton service | `@Service()` | `@angular/core` |
| Asynchronous data as signals | `resource()` | `@angular/core` |
| HTTP data as signals | `httpResource()` | `@angular/common/http` |
| Form | `form()`, `FormField`, `[formField]` | `@angular/forms/signals` |
| Dependency loaded on demand | `injectAsync()` | `@angular/core` |

[S3] [S4] [S7] [S8]

Wrong:

```ts
@Injectable({ providedIn: 'root' })
export class UserStore {
  public constructor(private readonly http: HttpClient) {}
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Service()
export class UserStore {
  private readonly http = inject(HttpClient);
}
```

New template syntax in 22: comments inside an opening tag; spread in object and array literals; several `@case` for one block, and `@default never;` to make a `@switch` exhaustive; short arrow functions such as `(click)="count.update(n => n + 1)"`. [S3]

## 5. Deprecated (Angular 22: do not write)

- `withFetch()` in `provideHttpClient()`. Remove it. [S4]
- The `reportProgress` request option. Use `reportUploadProgress` and `reportDownloadProgress`. [S4]
- The Webpack builders: `@angular-devkit/build-angular`, `@angular-devkit/build-webpack`, `@ngtools/webpack`. Use the builders of `@angular/build`. [S5]
- `CommonEngine` for server rendering. Use `AngularNodeAppEngine` or `AngularAppEngine`. [S5]

## 6. Migrations (Angular 22 update)

Update from 21 with `ng update @angular/core@22 @angular/cli@22`. Do not skip a major version. The update does these changes; do not redo them by hand: [S4] [S5]

- It adds `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Eager` to existing components where needed.
- It adds the `currentSnapshot` parameter for `CanMatchFn`, and `strictTemplates` to the TypeScript configuration.
- It keeps the XHR backend where the application reports upload progress.

Optional: `ng generate @angular/core:service`, from 22.1, turns `@Injectable` into `@Service`. [S6]

## Old patterns

- `changeDetection: ChangeDetectionStrategy.OnPush` (before 22): the opt-in to OnPush. Do not write it.
- `@Injectable({providedIn: 'root'})` (before 22): use `@Service()` in new code.
- `provideHttpClient(withFetch())` (before 22): do not write `withFetch()`.
- `import {form, Field} from '@angular/forms/signals'` with `[field]` (21.0): the experimental name. Write `FormField` and `[formField]`.

## Sources

- [S1] Angular Team, "Versioning and releases", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/releases>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Angular Team, "Announcing Angular v22", 2026-06-03, <https://blog.angular.dev/announcing-angular-v22-c52bb83a4664>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] angular/angular, "Release 22.0.0", <https://github.com/angular/angular/releases/tag/v22.0.0>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S5] angular/angular-cli, "Release 22.0.0", <https://github.com/angular/angular-cli/releases/tag/v22.0.0>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S6] Angular Team, "Injectable to Service migration", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/migrations/injectable-to-service>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S7] Angular Team, "Forms with signals", v22, <https://angular.dev/essentials/signal-forms>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S8] Types of `@angular/core` 22.2.6 installed in the example project of this base: `Service`, `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Eager` and `injectAsync` checked on 2026-11-13, MIT.

tool: node
version: "26"

# Node.js versions and built-in APIs

Contents: 1. Versions · 2. Running a TypeScript file · 3. Built-in APIs · Sources

## 1. Versions (Node.js LTS)

| Node.js | Released | State on 2026-11-12 | Angular that accepts it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 26 | 2026-05-05 | LTS | 22 |
| 24 | 2025-05-06 | LTS | 20, 21; 22 from 24.15 |
| 22 | 2024-04-24 | LTS | 18 to 21; 22 from 22.22.3 |
| 20 | 2023-04-17 | End of life | 17 to 21 |

Use only LTS versions in production. From version 27, one major version a year, and every version becomes LTS. [S1] [S2]

## 2. Running a TypeScript file directly (Node.js type stripping)

From Node.js 22.18, `node file.ts` runs a TypeScript file with no option. Node.js replaces the types by spaces: it checks no type and ignores `tsconfig.json`. [S3]

- Refused: `enum`, a `namespace` with code, parameter properties in a constructor, decorators, `.tsx` files.
- Write the extension in each import: `import { x } from './file.ts'`. Write `import type` for a type.
- A `.ts` file under `node_modules` is refused.

Wrong:

```ts
enum Mode { Fix, Check }
import { checkBase } from './check-base';
```

Right:

```ts
const MODES = ['fix', 'check'] as const;
import { checkBase } from './check-base.ts';
```

## 3. Built-in APIs that replace packages (Node.js 22 to 26)

| API | Since | Replaces |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `node --watch` | 22, stable | A tool that restarts on change |
| `glob`, `globSync` in `node:fs` | 22 | A file pattern package |
| `WebSocket` (client) | 22 | A WebSocket client package |
| `fetch`, `util.parseArgs`, `node:test` and `node --test` | 22 at most: the scripts and tests of this base use them with 22.18 | An HTTP client, an argument parser, a test runner |
| `require()` of an ES module | 22.12 | CommonJS and ES module workarounds |
| `Temporal` | 26 | A date library |

[S4] [S2] [S5]

## Sources

- [S1] Node.js, "Releases", <https://nodejs.org/en/about/previous-releases>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Node.js, "Modules: TypeScript", v26, <https://nodejs.org/api/typescript.html>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S4] Node.js, "Node.js 22 is now available!", <https://nodejs.org/en/blog/announcements/v22-release-announce>, and "Node.js 26.0.0", <https://nodejs.org/en/blog/release/v26.0.0>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] Scripts and tests of this base, run with Node.js 22.18.0 and 24.18.0 on 2026-11-13.

id: typescript-rxjs-and-signals
title: RxJS 7 and Angular signals
topic: typescript
summary: RxJS 7 deprecations, the bridge between signals and observables, and when to use each. RxJS 7.
tool: rxjs
version: "7.8"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S1, S2, S3]
license: MIT, written for this base; facts summarized from the RxJS types (Apache 2.0) and the Angular documentation (CC BY 4.0)
variant: chat
---

# RxJS 7 and Angular signals

Contents: 1. RxJS stays at 7 · 2. Deprecated in RxJS 7 · 3. Signals and observables · 4. The bridge · Sources

## 1. RxJS stays at 7 (RxJS versions)

The current version is 7.8.2. Version 8 is paused, with no release date: write RxJS 7 code. Angular 16 to 22 accept `^6.5.3 || ^7.4.0`. [S1] [S2]

## 2. Deprecated in RxJS 7 (RxJS 7 deprecations)

| Old pattern | Write instead |
| --- | --- |
| `source$.toPromise()` | `await firstValueFrom(source$)` or `await lastValueFrom(source$)` |
| `subscribe(next, error, complete)` | `subscribe({ next, error, complete })` |
| `Observable.create(…)` | `new Observable(…)` |

[S1]

Wrong:

```ts
const valves = await this.http.get<unknown>('/api/valves').toPromise();
```

Right:

```ts
const valves = await firstValueFrom(this.http.get<unknown>('/api/valves'));
```

## 3. Signals for state, observables for streams (Angular signals and RxJS)

- State of a component, and values derived from it: signals (`signal`, `computed`). [S3]
- Events over time, cancellation, combination of streams (messages, timers, sockets): observables. [S3]
- An observable in a template: the `async` pipe, or `toSignal()` in the component.
- A simple HTTP request, from Angular 22: `httpResource()`. Before 22: `HttpClient`.

## 4. The bridge between signals and observables (rxjs-interop)

| API, from `@angular/core/rxjs-interop` | Role | To know |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `toSignal(obs$, { initialValue })` | Observable to signal | Subscribes at once; needs the injection context, so call it in a field; without `initialValue`, gives `undefined` until the first value |
| `toObservable(signal)` | Signal to observable | Several synchronous writes give one value only |
| `rxResource({ params, stream })` | Resource fed by an observable | Same state signals as `resource` |
| `takeUntilDestroyed()` | End a subscription with the component | Without an argument, only in a field or the constructor |

[S3]

## Sources

- [S1] ReactiveX, types of `rxjs` 7.8.2 (`@deprecated` tags), <https://app.unpkg.com/rxjs@7.8.2/files/dist/types/internal/Observable.d.ts>, and the roadmap issue 6367, <https://github.com/ReactiveX/rxjs/issues/6367>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Angular Team, "RxJS interop with Angular signals", v22, <https://angular.dev/ecosystem/rxjs-interop>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

id: typescript-typescript-changes
title: "TypeScript 5.0 to 7.0: what changed"
topic: typescript
summary: Syntax added from 5.0 to 5.9, new defaults and removed options of 6.0, and what 7.0 changes. TypeScript 6.0.
tool: typescript
version: "6.0"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S1, S2, S3, S4]
license: MIT, written for this base; facts summarized from the TypeScript release notes (CC BY 4.0)
variant: chat
---

# TypeScript 5.0 to 7.0: what changed

Contents: 1. Version rule · 2. Syntax from 5.0 to 5.9 · 3. TypeScript 6.0 · 4. TypeScript 7.0 · Sources

## 1. Version rule (TypeScript versions)

Read the version of `typescript` in `package.json`. Use a syntax only when that version is equal to or higher than the version that added it. [S1]

In an Angular 22 project, `typescript` stays at 6.0: Angular 22 and typescript-eslint 8 accept `>=6.0.0 <6.1.0`. Never move it to 7 when you update dependencies. [S2] [S4]

## 2. Syntax added from 5.0 to 5.9 (TypeScript 5)

| Version | Use | Breaking change to know |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 5.0 | Standard decorators; `const` type parameters; `extends` with an array in `tsconfig`; `moduleResolution: bundler`; `verbatimModuleSyntax`; `export type *` | `target: ES3`, `out`, `importsNotUsedAsValues` deprecated |
| 5.2 | `using` and `await using` for disposable resources | `module` and `moduleResolution` must match for `node16` and `nodenext` |
| 5.3 | Import attributes: `import data from './a.json' with { type: 'json' }` | `super.x` on an instance field is an error |
| 5.4 | `NoInfer<T>`; narrowing kept in closures; `Object.groupBy`; `module: preserve` | Stricter assignment between enums |
| 5.5 | Inferred type predicates: `values.filter((v) => v !== undefined)` narrows the type | Options deprecated in 5.0 have no effect |
| 5.6 | Conditions always true or always false are errors; iterator helper methods | `.tsbuildinfo` always written |
| 5.8 | `erasableSyntaxOnly`: only syntax that Node.js can strip | `assert` in an import is refused under `nodenext` |
| 5.9 | `import defer`; `module: node20` | `ArrayBuffer` is no longer a supertype of `Buffer` |

[S1]

## 3. TypeScript 6.0 (released 2026-03-23)

New defaults: `strict: true`, `module: esnext`, `target` the latest ECMAScript (`es2025`), `types: []`, `rootDir` the folder of `tsconfig.json`, `noUncheckedSideEffectImports: true`. [S3]

| Old option or syntax | State in 6.0 | Write instead |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `"moduleResolution": "node"` or `"node10"` | Deprecated | `bundler` or `nodenext` |
| `"moduleResolution": "classic"`, `"outFile"` | Removed | `bundler`; ES modules |
| `"baseUrl"` | Deprecated | Full paths in `paths` |
| `"target": "es5"`, `"downlevelIteration"` | Deprecated | ES2015 at least |
| `"module": "amd"`, `"umd"`, `"systemjs"`, `"none"` | Not supported | ES modules |
| `module Foo { }` | Error | `namespace Foo { }` |
| `import x from './a.json' assert { type: 'json' }` | Error | `with { type: 'json' }` |

- To silence the deprecations while you migrate: `"ignoreDeprecations": "6.0"`. Remove it once done. [S3]
- New types: `Temporal`, `Map.prototype.getOrInsert`, `RegExp.escape`. [S3]

## 4. TypeScript 7.0 (released 2026-07-08)

- The compiler is native: builds are 7 to 12 times faster. `npm install -D typescript` installs it. [S4]
- It has no programming API before 7.1: Angular and typescript-eslint cannot use it yet. [S4]
- The options deprecated in 6.0 are errors. [S4]
- The 6.0 compiler stays available as `@typescript/typescript6`, command `tsc6`. [S4]

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "TypeScript 5.0" to "TypeScript 5.9" release notes, <https://typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-0.html> and the pages of the following versions, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] D. Rosenwasser, "Announcing TypeScript 6.0", 2026-03-23, <https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-6-0/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] D. Rosenwasser, "Announcing TypeScript 7.0", 2026-07-08, <https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-7-0/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
